{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017aJec3nD3JxNP5ynJkQdzv","type":"tool_result","content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"{\\\\\\\"text\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"Anarchism is a political philosophy and movement that is skeptical of all justifications for authority and seeks to abolish the institutions it claims maintain unnecessary coercion and hierarchy, typically including nation-states, and capitalism. Anarchism advocates for the replacement of the state with stateless societies and voluntary free associations. As a historically left-wing movement, this reading of anarchism is placed on the farthest left of the political spectrum, usually described as the libertarian wing of the socialist movement (libertarian socialism).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nHumans have lived in societies without formal hierarchies long before the establishment of states, realms, or empires. With the rise of organised hierarchical bodies, scepticism toward authority also rose. Although traces of anarchist ideas are found all throughout history, modern anarchism emerged from the Enlightenment. During the latter half of the 19th and the first decades of the 20th century, the anarchist movement flourished in most parts of the world and had a significant role in workers' struggles for emancipation. Various anarchist schools of thought formed during this period. Anarchists have taken part in several revolutions, most notably in the Paris Commune, the Russian Civil War and the Spanish Civil War, whose end marked the end of the classical era of anarchism. In the last decades of the 20th and into the 21st century, the anarchist movement has been resurgent once more, growing in popularity and influence within anti-capitalist, anti-war and anti-globalisation movements.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nAnarchists employ diverse approaches, which may be generally divided into revolutionary and evolutionary strategies; there is significant overlap between the two. Evolutionary methods try to simulate what an anarchist society might be like, but revolutionary tactics, which have historically taken a violent turn, aim to overthrow authority and the state. Many facets of human civilization have been influenced by anarchist theory, critique, and praxis.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nEtymology, terminology, and definition \\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe etymological origin of anarchism is from the Ancient Greek anarkhia, meaning \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"without a ruler\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", composed of the prefix an- (\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"without\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\") and the word arkhos (\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"leader\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"ruler\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"). The suffix -ism denotes the ideological current that favours anarchy. Anarchism appears in English from 1642 as anarchisme and anarchy from 1539; early English usages emphasised a sense of disorder. Various factions within the French Revolution labelled their opponents as anarchists, although few such accused shared many views with later anarchists. Many revolutionaries of the \\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"depth\\\\\\\": 0, \\\\\\\"source\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wikipedia\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"token_count\\\\\\\": 512, \\\\\\\"seed_id\\\\\\\": 0}\\\\n{\\\\\\\"text\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"Albedo (; ) is the fraction of sunlight that is diffusely reflected by a body. It is measured on a scale from 0 (corresponding to a black body that absorbs all incident radiation) to 1 (corresponding to a body that reflects all incident radiation).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nSurface albedo is defined as the ratio of radiosity Je to the irradiance Ee (flux per unit area) received by a surface. The proportion reflected is not only determined by properties of the surface itself, but also by the spectral and angular distribution of solar radiation reaching the Earth's surface. These factors vary with atmospheric composition, geographic location, and time (see position of the Sun). While bi-hemispherical reflectance is calculated for a single angle of incidence (i.e., for a given position of the Sun), albedo is the directional integration of reflectance over all solar angles in a given period. The temporal resolution may range from seconds (as obtained from flux measurements) to daily, monthly, or annual averages.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nUnless given for a specific wavelength (spectral albedo), albedo refers to the entire spectrum of solar radiation. Due to measurement constraints, it is often given for the spectrum in which most solar energy reaches the surface (between 0.3 and 3 μm). This spectrum includes visible light (0.4–0.7 μm), which explains why surfaces with a low albedo appear dark (e.g., trees absorb most radiation), whereas surfaces with a high albedo appear bright (e.g., snow reflects most radiation).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIce–albedo feedback is a positive feedback climate process where a change in the area of ice caps, glaciers, and sea ice alters the albedo and surface temperature of a planet. Ice is very reflective, therefore it reflects far more solar energy back to space than the other types of land area or open water. Ice–albedo feedback plays an important role in global climate change.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nAlbedo is an important concept in climatology, astronomy, and environmental management. The average albedo of the Earth from the upper atmosphere, its planetary albedo, is 30–35% because of cloud cover, but widely varies locally across the surface because of different geological and environmental features.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nTerrestrial albedo\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nAny albedo in visible light falls within a range of about 0.9 for fresh snow to about 0.04 for charcoal, one of the darkest substances. Deeply shadowed cavities can achieve an effective albedo approaching the zero of a black body\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"depth\\\\\\\": 0, \\\\\\\"source\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wikipedia\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"token_count\\\\\\\": 512, \\\\\\\"seed_id\\\\\\\": 1}\\\\n{\\\\\\\"text\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"A, or a, is the first letter and the first vowel of the Latin alphabet, used in the modern English alphabet, the alphabets of other western European languages and others worldwide. Its name in English is a (pronounced ), plural aes. It is similar in shape to the Ancient Greek letter Alpha, from which it derives. The uppercase version consists of the two slanting sides of a triangle, crossed in the middle by a horizontal bar. The lowercase version can be written in two forms: the double-storey a and single-storey ɑ. The latter is commonly used in handwriting and fonts based on it, especially fonts intended to be read by children, and is also found in italic type.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIn English grammar, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"a\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", and its variant \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"an\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", are indefinite articles.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nHistory \\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe earliest known certain ancestor of \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"A\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" is aleph (also written 'aleph), the first letter of the Phoenician alphabet, which consisted entirely of consonants (for that reason, it is also called an abjad to distinguish it from a true alphabet). In turn, the ancestor of aleph may have been a pictogram of an ox head in proto-Sinaitic script influenced by Egyptian hieroglyphs, styled as a triangular head with two horns extended.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhen the ancient Greeks adopted the alphabet, they had no use for a letter to represent the glottal stop—the consonant sound that the letter denoted in Phoenician and other Semitic languages, and that was the first phoneme of the Phoenician pronunciation of the letter—so they used their version of the sign to represent the vowel , and called it by the similar name of alpha. In the earliest Greek inscriptions after the Greek Dark Ages, dating to the eighth century BC, the letter rests upon its side, but in the Greek alphabet of later times it generally resembles the modern capital letter, although many local varieties can be distinguished by the shortening of one leg, or by the angle at which the cross line is set.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe Etruscans brought the Greek alphabet to their civilization in the Italian Peninsula and left the letter unchanged. The Romans later adopted the Etruscan alphabet to write the Latin language, and the resulting letter was preserved in the Latin alphabet that would come to be used to write many languages, including English.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nTypographic variants \\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nDuring Roman times, there were many variant forms of the letter \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"A\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\". First was the monumental or lapidary style, which was used when inscribing on stone or other\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"depth\\\\\\\": 0, \\\\\\\"source\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wikipedia\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"token_count\\\\\\\": 512, \\\\\\\"seed_id\\\\\\\": 2}\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.93}}\"}"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"82071bbc-f3ac-4309-af65-af2b1d1f083a","uuid":"301631ec-b614-493a-a51d-f64cb35d657d","timestamp":"2026-05-17T13:32:26.401Z","tool_use_result":{"content":"{\"result\":\"{\\\"success\\\": true, \\\"data\\\": {\\\"stdout\\\": \\\"{\\\\\\\"text\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"Anarchism is a political philosophy and movement that is skeptical of all justifications for authority and seeks to abolish the institutions it claims maintain unnecessary coercion and hierarchy, typically including nation-states, and capitalism. This spectrum includes visible light (0.4–0.7 μm), which explains why surfaces with a low albedo appear dark (e.g., trees absorb most radiation), whereas surfaces with a high albedo appear bright (e.g., snow reflects most radiation).\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIce–albedo feedback is a positive feedback climate process where a change in the area of ice caps, glaciers, and sea ice alters the albedo and surface temperature of a planet. Ice is very reflective, therefore it reflects far more solar energy back to space than the other types of land area or open water. Ice–albedo feedback plays an important role in global climate change.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nAlbedo is an important concept in climatology, astronomy, and environmental management. Deeply shadowed cavities can achieve an effective albedo approaching the zero of a black body\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"depth\\\\\\\": 0, \\\\\\\"source\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wikipedia\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"token_count\\\\\\\": 512, \\\\\\\"seed_id\\\\\\\": 1}\\\\n{\\\\\\\"text\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"A, or a, is the first letter and the first vowel of the Latin alphabet, used in the modern English alphabet, the alphabets of other western European languages and others worldwide. Its name in English is a (pronounced ), plural aes. It is similar in shape to the Ancient Greek letter Alpha, from which it derives. The uppercase version consists of the two slanting sides of a triangle, crossed in the middle by a horizontal bar. The lowercase version can be written in two forms: the double-storey a and single-storey ɑ. The latter is commonly used in handwriting and fonts based on it, especially fonts intended to be read by children, and is also found in italic type.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nIn English grammar, \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"a\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", and its variant \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"an\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\", are indefinite articles.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nHistory \\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe earliest known certain ancestor of \\\\\\\\\\\\\\\"A\\\\\\\\\\\\\\\" is aleph (also written 'aleph), the first letter of the Phoenician alphabet, which consisted entirely of consonants (for that reason, it is also called an abjad to distinguish it from a true alphabet). In turn, the ancestor of aleph may have been a pictogram of an ox head in proto-Sinaitic script influenced by Egyptian hieroglyphs, styled as a triangular head with two horns extended.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nWhen the ancient Greeks adopted the alphabet, they had no use for a letter to represent the glottal stop—the consonant sound that the letter denoted in Phoenician and other Semitic languages, and that was the first phoneme of the Phoenician pronunciation of the letter—so they used their version of the sign to represent the vowel , and called it by the similar name of alpha. In the earliest Greek inscriptions after the Greek Dark Ages, dating to the eighth century BC, the letter rests upon its side, but in the Greek alphabet of later times it generally resembles the modern capital letter, although many local varieties can be distinguished by the shortening of one leg, or by the angle at which the cross line is set.\\\\\\\\n\\\\\\\\nThe Etruscans brought the Greek alphabet to their civilization in the Italian Peninsula and left the letter unchanged. First was the monumental or lapidary style, which was used when inscribing on stone or other\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"depth\\\\\\\": 0, \\\\\\\"source\\\\\\\": \\\\\\\"wikipedia\\\\\\\", \\\\\\\"token_count\\\\\\\": 512, \\\\\\\"seed_id\\\\\\\": 2}\\\\n\\\", \\\"stderr\\\": \\\"\\\", \\\"return_code\\\": 0}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.93}}\"}","structuredContent":{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"{\\\"text\\\": \\\"Anarchism is a political philosophy and movement that is skeptical of all justifications for authority and seeks to abolish the institutions it claims maintain unnecessary coercion and hierarchy, typically including nation-states, and capitalism. Anarchism advocates for the replacement of the state with stateless societies and voluntary free associations. As a historically left-wing movement, this reading of anarchism is placed on the farthest left of the political spectrum, usually described as the libertarian wing of the socialist movement (libertarian socialism).\\\\n\\\\nHumans have lived in societies without formal hierarchies long before the establishment of states, realms, or empires. With the rise of organised hierarchical bodies, scepticism toward authority also rose. Although traces of anarchist ideas are found all throughout history, modern anarchism emerged from the Enlightenment. During the latter half of the 19th and the first decades of the 20th century, the anarchist movement flourished in most parts of the world and had a significant role in workers' struggles for emancipation. Various anarchist schools of thought formed during this period. Anarchists have taken part in several revolutions, most notably in the Paris Commune, the Russian Civil War and the Spanish Civil War, whose end marked the end of the classical era of anarchism. In the last decades of the 20th and into the 21st century, the anarchist movement has been resurgent once more, growing in popularity and influence within anti-capitalist, anti-war and anti-globalisation movements.\\\\n\\\\nAnarchists employ diverse approaches, which may be generally divided into revolutionary and evolutionary strategies; there is significant overlap between the two. Evolutionary methods try to simulate what an anarchist society might be like, but revolutionary tactics, which have historically taken a violent turn, aim to overthrow authority and the state. Many facets of human civilization have been influenced by anarchist theory, critique, and praxis.\\\\n\\\\nEtymology, terminology, and definition \\\\n\\\\nThe etymological origin of anarchism is from the Ancient Greek anarkhia, meaning \\\\\\\"without a ruler\\\\\\\", composed of the prefix an- (\\\\\\\"without\\\\\\\") and the word arkhos (\\\\\\\"leader\\\\\\\" or \\\\\\\"ruler\\\\\\\"). The suffix -ism denotes the ideological current that favours anarchy. Anarchism appears in English from 1642 as anarchisme and anarchy from 1539; early English usages emphasised a sense of disorder. Various factions within the French Revolution labelled their opponents as anarchists, although few such accused shared many views with later anarchists. Many revolutionaries of the \\\", \\\"depth\\\": 0, \\\"source\\\": \\\"wikipedia\\\", \\\"token_count\\\": 512, \\\"seed_id\\\": 0}\\n{\\\"text\\\": \\\"Albedo (; ) is the fraction of sunlight that is diffusely reflected by a body. It is measured on a scale from 0 (corresponding to a black body that absorbs all incident radiation) to 1 (corresponding to a body that reflects all incident radiation).\\\\n\\\\nSurface albedo is defined as the ratio of radiosity Je to the irradiance Ee (flux per unit area) received by a surface. The proportion reflected is not only determined by properties of the surface itself, but also by the spectral and angular distribution of solar radiation reaching the Earth's surface. These factors vary with atmospheric composition, geographic location, and time (see position of the Sun). While bi-hemispherical reflectance is calculated for a single angle of incidence (i.e., for a given position of the Sun), albedo is the directional integration of reflectance over all solar angles in a given period. The temporal resolution may range from seconds (as obtained from flux measurements) to daily, monthly, or annual averages.\\\\n\\\\nUnless given for a specific wavelength (spectral albedo), albedo refers to the entire spectrum of solar radiation. Ice is very reflective, therefore it reflects far more solar energy back to space than the other types of land area or open water. Ice–albedo feedback plays an important role in global climate change.\\\\n\\\\nAlbedo is an important concept in climatology, astronomy, and environmental management. \\\"def optimal_binning(scores, labels, n_bins=4):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Cut scores into n_bins using training quantiles, return accuracy.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    thresholds = np.quantile(scores, [i / n_bins for i in range(1, n_bins)])\\\\n    preds = np.digitize(scores, thresholds)\\\\n    return accuracy_score(labels, preds), preds\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef lr_calibration_cv(scores, labels, n_splits=5, seed=42):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"5-fold CV logistic regression on single score feature.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    X = scores.reshape(-1, 1)\\\\n    skf = StratifiedKFold(n_splits=n_splits, shuffle=True, random_state=seed)\\\\n    accs = []\\\\n    all_preds = np.zeros_like(labels)\\\\n\\\\n    for train_idx, test_idx in skf.split(X, labels):\\\\n        clf = LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=seed, multi_class=\\\\\\\"multinomial\\\\\\\")\\\\n        clf.fit(X[train_idx], labels[train_idx])\\\\n        preds = clf.predict(X[test_idx])\\\\n        all_preds[test_idx] = preds\\\\n        accs.append(accuracy_score(labels[test_idx], preds))\\\\n\\\\n    return np.mean(accs), np.std(accs), all_preds\\\\n\\\\n\\\\ndef run_cell(cell_name, data_dir, model_obs, model_perf, tok_obs, tok_perf, device, batch_size):\\\\n    \\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"Run all scoring methods on one cell.\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\"\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"\\\\\\\\n{'='*60}\\\\\\\")\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"  Processing: {cell_name}\\\\\\\")\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"{'='*60}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    texts, labels = load_cell_data(data_dir)\\\\n    if len(texts) == 0:\\\\n        return None\\\\n    n = len(texts)\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"  Total: {n} samples, depths: {np.bincount(labels).tolist()}\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    t0 = time.time()\\\\n    print(\\\\\\\"  Computing log-likelihoods (observer: gpt2-xl)...\\\\\\\")\\\\n    ll_obs = compute_log_likelihoods(model_obs, tok_obs, texts, device, batch_size)\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"    Done in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    t1 = time.time()\\\\n    print(\\\\\\\"  Computing log-likelihoods (performer: pythia-1.4b)...\\\\\\\")\\\\n    ll_perf = compute_log_likelihoods(model_perf, tok_perf, texts, device, batch_size)\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"    Done in {time.time()-t1:.1f}s\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    t2 = time.time()\\\\n    print(\\\\\\\"  Computing Fast-DetectGPT scores (gpt2-xl)...\\\\\\\")\\\\n    fdgpt_scores = compute_fast_detectgpt_scores(model_obs, tok_obs, texts, device, batch_size)\\\\n    print(f\\\\\\\"    Done in {time.time()-t2:.1f}s\\\\\\\")\\\\n\\\\n    # Binoculars score: CE_observer / CE_performer\\\\n    ce_obs = -ll_obs\\\\n    ce_perf = -ll_perf\\\\n    bino_scores = ce_obs / np.clip(ce_perf, 1e-8, None)\\\\n\\\\n    results = {\\\\\\\"cell\\\\\\\": cell_name, \\\\\\\"n_samples\\\\\\\": n}\\\\n\\\\n    # Binoculars calibration\\\\n    bino_bin_acc, _ = optimal_binning(bino_scores, labels)\\\\n    bino_lr_acc, bino_lr_std, _ = lr_calibration_cv(bino_scores, labels)\\\\n    results[\\\\\\\"binoculars\\\\\\\"] = {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"binning_acc\\\\\\\": round(float(bino_bin_acc), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"lr_acc_mean\\\\\\\": round(float(bino_lr_acc), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"lr_acc_std\\\\\\\": round(float(bino_lr_std), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"score_stats\\\\\\\": {\\\\n            f\\\\\\\"d{d}\\\\\\\": {\\\\\\\"mean\\\\\\\": round(float(bino_scores[labels == d].mean()), 4),\\\\n                      \\\\\\\"std\\\\\\\": round(float(bino_scores[labels == d].std()), 4)}\\\\n            for d in range(NUM_DEPTHS)\\\\n        }\\\\n    }\\\\n\\\\n    # Fast-DetectGPT calibration\\\\n    fdgpt_bin_acc, _ = optimal_binning(fdgpt_scores, labels)\\\\n    fdgpt_lr_acc, fdgpt_lr_std, _ = lr_calibration_cv(fdgpt_scores, labels)\\\\n    results[\\\\\\\"fast_detectgpt\\\\\\\"] = {\\\\n        \\\\\\\"binning_acc\\\\\\\": round(float(fdgpt_bin_acc), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"lr_acc_mean\\\\\\\": round(float(fdgpt_lr_acc), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"lr_acc_std\\\\\\\": round(float(fdgpt_lr_std), 4),\\\\n        \\\\\\\"score_stats\\\\\\\": {\\\\n            f\\\\\\\"d{d}\\\\\\\": {\\\\\\\"mean\\\\\\\": round(float(fdgpt_scores[labels == d].mean()), 4),\\\\n                      \\\\\\\"std\\\\\\\": round(float(fdgpt_scores[labels == d].std()), 4)}\\\\n            for d in range(NUM_DEPTHS)\\\\n {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Cv2RPugoyDh99pJZTrhRWW","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01THq4riBjNp5xZFLux8JMUu","name":"mcp__agent-ml-research__ssh_execute","input":{"server":"gpu-6000pro-2","command":"cat > /root/autodl-tmp/recursive_gen_depth_est/scripts/detector_baselines/run_all_detectors.py << 'PYEOF'\n\"\"\"\nDetection-based baselines for recursive generation depth estimation.\n\nComputes 5 continuous detection scores on 3x3 grid data, then evaluates\nordinal depth prediction via LR, RF, and quantile binning.\n\nDetectors:\n  1. Binoculars: CE_observer / CE_performer (gpt2-xl / pythia-1.4b)\n  2. Fast-DetectGPT: normalized (token_ll + entropy) discrepancy\n  3. Log-Rank: average log-rank of actual tokens under scoring model\n  4. Entropy: average predictive entropy\n  5. LLR: log-likelihood ratio (ll_observer - ll_performer)\n\nEvaluation:\n  - 5-fold CV Logistic Regression (per-score + combined)\n  - 5-fold CV Random Forest (per-score + combined)\n  - Quantile binning\n  - Pairwise AUC: d0v1, d1v2, d2v3\n\nUsage:\n  python scripts/detector_baselines/run_all_detectors.py --gpu 0\n  python scripts/detector_baselines/run_all_detectors.py --gpu 0 --pilot\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport os\nimport sys\nimport time\nimport numpy as np\nimport torch\nfrom torch.utils.data import DataLoader, Dataset\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModelForCausalLM\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier\nfrom sklearn.model_selection import StratifiedKFold\nfrom sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, roc_auc_score\n\nPROJECT_ROOT = \"/root/autodl-tmp/recursive_gen_depth_est\"\n\nGRID_CELLS = {\n    \"pythia_nucleus\": \"data/\",\n    \"pythia_temp09\": \"data/pythia_temp09/\",\n    \"pythia_topk50\": \"data/pythia_topk50/\",\n    \"olmo_nucleus\": \"data/olmo/\",\n    \"olmo_temp09\": \"data/olmo_temp09/\",\n    \"olmo_topk50\": \"data/olmo_topk50/\",\n    \"gpt2xl_nucleus\": \"data/gpt2xl/\",\n    \"gpt2xl_temp09\": \"data/gpt2xl_temp09/\",\n    \"gpt2xl_topk50\": \"data/gpt2xl_topk50/\",\n}\n\nMODEL_PATHS = {\n    \"gpt2xl\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/models/gpt2-xl\",\n    \"pythia\": \"/root/autodl-tmp/models/pythia-1.4b\",\n}\n\nNUM_DEPTHS = 4\nMAX_SEQ_LEN = 512\n\n\nclass TextDataset(Dataset):\n    def __init__(self, texts, tokenizer, max_len=512):\n        self.encodings = tokenizer(\n            texts, max_length=max_len, truncation=True,\n            padding=\"max_length\", return_tensors=\"pt\"\n        )\n\n    def __len__(self):\n        return self.encodings[\"input_ids\"].shape[0]\n\n    def __getitem__(self, idx):\n        return {k: v[idx] for k, v in self.encodings.items()}\n\n\ndef load_cell_data(data_dir, max_per_depth=5000):\n    texts, labels = [], []\n    for d in range(NUM_DEPTHS):\n        fpath = os.path.join(data_dir, f\"depth_{d}.jsonl\")\n        if not os.path.exists(fpath):\n            print(f\"  WARNING: {fpath} not found, skipping depth {d}\")\n            continue\n        count = 0\n        with open(fpath) as f:\n            for line in f:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line:\n                    continue\n                rec = json.loads(line)\n                texts.append(rec[\"text\"])\n                labels.append(d)\n                count += 1\n                if count >= max_per_depth:\n                    break\n        print(f\"  depth_{d}: {count} samples\")\n    return texts, np.array(labels)\n\n\n@torch.no_grad()\ndef compute_all_scores(model, tokenizer, texts, device, batch_size=16, label=\"model\"):\n    \"\"\"Compute log-likelihood, entropy, log-rank, and fast-detectgpt scores in one pass.\"\"\"\n    dataset = TextDataset(texts, tokenizer, MAX_SEQ_LEN)\n    loader = DataLoader(dataset, batch_size=batch_size, shuffle=False)\n\n    all_ll = []\n    all_entropy = []\n    all_logrank = []\n    all_fdgpt = []\n\n    for i, batch in enumerate(loader):\n        input_ids = batch[\"input_ids\"].to(device)\n        attention_mask = batch[\"attention_mask\"].to(device)\n\n        outputs = model(input_ids=input_ids, attention_mask=attention_mask)\n        logits = outputs.logits[:, :-1, :]\n        targets = input_ids[:, 1:]\n        mask = attention_mask[:, 1:].float()\n        lengths = mask.sum(dim=1).clamp(min=1)\n\n        log_probs = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1)\n        probs = torch.softmax(logits, dim=-1)\n\n        # 1. Log-likelihood\n        token_ll = log_probs.gather(2, targets.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n        token_ll_masked = token_ll * mask\n        mean_ll = token_ll_masked.sum(dim=1) / lengths\n        all_ll.append(mean_ll.cpu().numpy())\n\n        # 2. Entropy\n        entropy = -(probs * log_probs).sum(dim=-1)\n        entropy_masked = entropy * mask\n        mean_entropy = entropy_masked.sum(dim=1) / lengths\n        all_entropy.append(mean_entropy.cpu().numpy())\n\n        # 3. Log-Rank: rank of actual token in sorted probability distribution\n        # Sort probabilities descending, find where actual token falls\n        target_probs = probs.gather(2, targets.unsqueeze(-1)).squeeze(-1)\n        # Rank = number of tokens with higher probability + 1\n        ranks = (probs > target_probs.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(dim=-1).float() + 1.0\n        log_ranks = torch.log(ranks)\n        log_ranks_masked = log_ranks * mask\n        mean_logrank = log_ranks_masked.sum(dim=1) / lengths\n        all_logrank.append(mean_logrank.cpu().numpy())\n\n        # 4. Fast-DetectGPT: normalized discrepancy\n        discrepancy = token_ll + entropy\n        discrepancy_masked = discrepancy * mask\n        mean_d = discrepancy_masked.sum(dim=1) / lengths\n        sum_sq = ((discrepancy - mean_d.unsqueeze(1)) ** 2 * mask).sum(dim=1)\n        std_d = (sum_sq / lengths.clamp(min=2)).sqrt().clamp(min=1e-8)\n        fdgpt_score = mean_d / std_d\n        all_fdgpt.append(fdgpt_score.cpu().numpy())\n\n        if (i + 1) % 50 == 0:\n            print(f\"    [{label}] batch {i+1}/{len(loader)}\")\n\n    return {\n        \"ll\": np.concatenate(all_ll),\n        \"entropy\": np.concatenate(all_entropy),\n        \"logrank\": np.concatenate(all_logrank),\n        \"fdgpt\": np.concatenate(all_fdgpt),\n    }\n\n\ndef quantile_binning(scores, labels, n_bins=4):\n    thresholds = np.quantile(scores, [i / n_bins for i in range(1, n_bins)])\n    preds = np.digitize(scores, thresholds)\n    return accuracy_score(labels, preds)\n\n\ndef cv_evaluate(X, labels, clf_factory, n_splits=5, seed=42):\n    \"\"\"5-fold CV evaluation, returns mean acc, std, and OOF predictions.\"\"\"\n    skf = StratifiedKFold(n_splits=n_splits, shuffle=True, random_state=seed)\n    accs = []\n    oof_preds = np.zeros_like(labels)\n    oof_probs = np.zeros((len(labels), NUM_DEPTHS))\n\n    for train_idx, test_idx in skf.split(X, labels):\n        clf = clf_factory()\n        clf.fit(X[train_idx], labels[train_idx])\n        preds = clf.predict(X[test_idx])\n        oof_preds[test_idx] = preds\n        if hasattr(clf, \"predict_proba\"):\n            oof_probs[test_idx] = clf.predict_proba(X[test_idx])\n        accs.append(accuracy_score(labels[test_idx], preds))\n\n    return {\n        \"acc_mean\": round(float(np.mean(accs)), 4),\n        \"acc_std\": round(float(np.std(accs)), 4),\n        \"oof_preds\": oof_preds,\n        \"oof_probs\": oof_probs,\n    }\n\n\ndef pairwise_auc(scores, labels, d_low, d_high):\n    \"\"\"Binary AUC between two depths using raw continuous score.\"\"\"\n    mask = (labels == d_low) | (labels == d_high)\n    if mask.sum() < 10:\n        return None\n    y = (labels[mask] == d_high).astype(int)\n    s = scores[mask]\n    try:\n        auc = roc_auc_score(y, s)\n        return round(float(max(auc, 1 - auc)), 4)  # direction-agnostic\n    except ValueError:\n        return None\n\n\ndef score_stats(scores, labels):\n    return {\n        f\"d{d}\": {\"mean\": round(float(scores[labels == d].mean()), 4),\n                   \"std\": round(float(scores[labels == d].std()), 4)}\n        for d in range(NUM_DEPTHS) if (labels == d).sum() > 0\n    }\n\n\ndef evaluate_single_score(name, scores, labels):\n    \"\"\"Full evaluation suite for one continuous score.\"\"\"\n    X = scores.reshape(-1, 1)\n\n    lr_result = cv_evaluate(\n        X, labels,\n        lambda: LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=42, multi_class=\"multinomial\")\n    )\n    rf_result = cv_evaluate(\n        X, labels,\n        lambda: RandomForestClassifier(n_estimators=100, random_state=42, n_jobs=-1)\n    )\n    bin_acc = quantile_binning(scores, labels)\n\n    aucs = {\n        \"d0v1\": pairwise_auc(scores, labels, 0, 1),\n        \"d1v2\": pairwise_auc(scores, labels, 1, 2),\n        \"d2v3\": pairwise_auc(scores, labels, 2, 3),\n    }\n\n    return {\n        \"lr_acc_mean\": lr_result[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"lr_acc_std\": lr_result[\"acc_std\"],\n        \"rf_acc_mean\": rf_result[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"rf_acc_std\": rf_result[\"acc_std\"],\n        \"binning_acc\": round(float(bin_acc), 4),\n        \"pairwise_auc\": aucs,\n        \"score_stats\": score_stats(scores, labels),\n    }\n\n\ndef run_cell(cell_name, data_dir, model_obs, model_perf, tok_obs, tok_perf, device, batch_size):\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*60}\")\n    print(f\"  Processing: {cell_name}\")\n    print(f\"{'='*60}\")\n\n    texts, labels = load_cell_data(data_dir)\n    if len(texts) == 0:\n        return None\n    n = len(texts)\n    print(f\"  Total: {n} samples, depths: {np.bincount(labels).tolist()}\")\n\n    t0 = time.time()\n    print(\"  Scoring with observer (gpt2-xl)...\")\n    obs_scores = compute_all_scores(model_obs, tok_obs, texts, device, batch_size, \"gpt2xl\")\n    print(f\"    Done in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    t1 = time.time()\n    print(\"  Scoring with performer (pythia-1.4b)...\")\n    perf_scores = compute_all_scores(model_perf, tok_perf, texts, device, batch_size, \"pythia\")\n    print(f\"    Done in {time.time()-t1:.1f}s\")\n\n    # Derive detector scores\n    ce_obs = -obs_scores[\"ll\"]\n    ce_perf = -perf_scores[\"ll\"]\n    bino_scores = ce_obs / np.clip(ce_perf, 1e-8, None)\n    llr_scores = obs_scores[\"ll\"] - perf_scores[\"ll\"]\n    fdgpt_scores = obs_scores[\"fdgpt\"]\n    logrank_scores = obs_scores[\"logrank\"]\n    entropy_scores = obs_scores[\"entropy\"]\n\n    detector_scores = {\n        \"binoculars\": bino_scores,\n        \"fast_detectgpt\": fdgpt_scores,\n        \"log_rank\": logrank_scores,\n        \"entropy\": entropy_scores,\n        \"llr\": llr_scores,\n    }\n\n    results = {\"cell\": cell_name, \"n_samples\": n}\n\n    # Evaluate each detector individually\n    for det_name, det_scores in detector_scores.items():\n        print(f\"  Evaluating {det_name}...\")\n        results[det_name] = evaluate_single_score(det_name, det_scores, labels)\n\n    # Combined: all 5 scores as features\n    X_all = np.column_stack([v for v in detector_scores.values()])\n    print(\"  Evaluating combined (5 features)...\")\n    lr_comb = cv_evaluate(\n        X_all, labels,\n        lambda: LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=42, multi_class=\"multinomial\")\n    )\n    rf_comb = cv_evaluate(\n        X_all, labels,\n        lambda: RandomForestClassifier(n_estimators=200, random_state=42, n_jobs=-1)\n    )\n    results[\"combined_5feat\"] = {\n        \"lr_acc_mean\": lr_comb[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"lr_acc_std\": lr_comb[\"acc_std\"],\n        \"rf_acc_mean\": rf_comb[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"rf_acc_std\": rf_comb[\"acc_std\"],\n    }\n\n    # Binoculars + Fast-DetectGPT + Log-Rank (3 core detectors)\n    X_3det = np.column_stack([bino_scores, fdgpt_scores, logrank_scores])\n    lr_3 = cv_evaluate(\n        X_3det, labels,\n        lambda: LogisticRegression(max_iter=1000, random_state=42, multi_class=\"multinomial\")\n    )\n    rf_3 = cv_evaluate(\n        X_3det, labels,\n        lambda: RandomForestClassifier(n_estimators=200, random_state=42, n_jobs=-1)\n    )\n    results[\"combined_3det\"] = {\n        \"lr_acc_mean\": lr_3[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"lr_acc_std\": lr_3[\"acc_std\"],\n        \"rf_acc_mean\": rf_3[\"acc_mean\"],\n        \"rf_acc_std\": rf_3[\"acc_std\"],\n    }\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - t0\n    results[\"elapsed_seconds\"] = round(elapsed, 1)\n    print(f\"  Cell done in {elapsed:.1f}s\")\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef main():\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    parser.add_argument(\"--gpu\", type=int, default=0)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--batch_size\", type=int, default=16)\n    parser.add_argument(\"--pilot\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Only run pythia_nucleus\")\n    parser.add_argument(\"--output_dir\", default=os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, \"results/detector_baselines\"))\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    device = torch.device(f\"cuda:{args.gpu}\")\n    os.makedirs(args.output_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\n    print(\"Loading models...\")\n    t0 = time.time()\n    tok_obs = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_PATHS[\"gpt2xl\"])\n    tok_obs.pad_token = tok_obs.eos_token\n    model_obs = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        MODEL_PATHS[\"gpt2xl\"], torch_dtype=torch.float16\n    ).to(device).eval()\n    print(f\"  gpt2-xl loaded in {time.time()-t0:.1f}s\")\n\n    t1 = time.time()\n    tok_perf = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_PATHS[\"pythia\"])\n    tok_perf.pad_token = tok_perf.eos_token\n    model_perf = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\n        MODEL_PATHS[\"pythia\"], torch_dtype=torch.float16\n    ).to(device).eval()\n    print(f\"  pythia-1.4b loaded in {time.time()-t1:.1f}s\")\n\n    cells = {\"pythia_nucleus\": GRID_CELLS[\"pythia_nucleus\"]} if args.pilot else GRID_CELLS\n    all_results = {}\n    start = time.time()\n\n    for cell_name, data_dir in cells.items():\n        full_dir = os.path.join(PROJECT_ROOT, data_dir)\n        result = run_cell(cell_name, full_dir, model_obs, model_perf,\n                         tok_obs, tok_perf, device, args.batch_size)\n        if result:\n            all_results[cell_name] = result\n\n    elapsed = time.time() - start\n\n    rf_reference = {\n        \"pythia_nucleus\": 0.7813, \"pythia_temp09\": 0.7677, \"pythia_topk50\": 0.687,\n        \"olmo_nucleus\": 0.7877, \"olmo_temp09\": 0.827, \"olmo_topk50\": 0.737,\n        \"gpt2xl_nucleus\": 0.8397, \"gpt2xl_temp09\": 0.8527, \"gpt2xl_topk50\": 0.754,\n    }\n\n    detectors = [\"binoculars\", \"fast_detectgpt\", \"log_rank\", \"entropy\", \"llr\"]\n\n    # Print comparison table\n    print(f\"\\n\\n{'='*120}\")\n    print(\"  COMPARISON TABLE: 4-class Accuracy (best of LR/RF per detector)\")\n    print(f\"{'='*120}\")\n    header = f\"  {'Cell':<18} {'RF-19feat':<11}\"\n    for d in detectors:\n        header += f\" {d[:10]:<11}\"\n    header += f\" {'Comb-5':<11} {'Comb-3det':<11}\"\n    print(header)\n    print(f\"  {'-'*18} \" + f\"{'-'*11} \" * (len(detectors) + 3))\n\n    for cell_name in cells:\n        if cell_name not in all_results:\n            continue\n        r = all_results[cell_name]\n        rf = rf_reference.get(cell_name, 0)\n        row = f\"  {cell_name:<18} {rf:<11.4f}\"\n        for d in detectors:\n            best = max(r[d][\"lr_acc_mean\"], r[d][\"rf_acc_mean\"])\n            row += f\" {best:<11.4f}\"\n        c5 = max(r[\"combined_5feat\"][\"lr_acc_mean\"], r[\"combined_5feat\"][\"rf_acc_mean\"])\n        c3 = max(r[\"combined_3det\"][\"lr_acc_mean\"], r[\"combined_3det\"][\"rf_acc_mean\"])\n        row += f\" {c5:<11.4f} {c3:<11.4f}\"\n        print(row)\n\n    # Print AUC table\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*100}\")\n    print(\"  PAIRWISE AUC TABLE\")\n    print(f\"{'='*100}\")\n    for pair in [\"d0v1\", \"d1v2\", \"d2v3\"]:\n        print(f\"\\n  {pair}:\")\n        row = f\"  {'Cell':<18}\"\n        for d in detectors:\n            row += f\" {d[:10]:<11}\"\n        print(row)\n        for cell_name in cells:\n            if cell_name not in all_results:\n                continue\n            r = all_results[cell_name]\n            row = f\"  {cell_name:<18}\"\n            for d in detectors:\n                v = r[d][\"pairwise_auc\"].get(pair)\n                row += f\" {v if v else 'N/A':<11}\"\n            print(row)\n\n    # Build summary\n    summary = {\n        \"experiment\": \"detector_baselines_v2\",\n        \"detectors\": detectors,\n        \"models\": {\n            \"observer\": \"gpt2-xl\",\n            \"performer\": \"pythia-1.4b\",\n        },\n        \"per_cell\": all_results,\n        \"rf_reference\": rf_reference,\n        \"elapsed_seconds\": round(elapsed, 1),\n        \"pilot_mode\": args.pilot,\n    }\n\n    # Compute overall means\n    means = {}\n    for d in detectors:\n        lr_accs = [all_results[c][d][\"lr_acc_mean\"] for c in all_results]\n        rf_accs = [all_results[c][d][\"rf_acc_mean\"] for c in all_results]\n        best_accs = [max(l, r) for l, r in zip(lr_accs, rf_accs)]\n        means[d] = {\n            \"mean_lr_acc\": round(float(np.mean(lr_accs)), 4),\n            \"mean_rf_acc\": round(float(np.mean(rf_accs)), 4),\n            \"mean_best_acc\": round(float(np.mean(best_accs)), 4),\n        }\n    summary[\"means\"] = means\n\n    c5_accs = [max(all_results[c][\"combined_5feat\"][\"lr_acc_mean\"],\n                   all_results[c][\"combined_5feat\"][\"rf_acc_mean\"]) for c in all_results]\n    c3_accs = [max(all_results[c][\"combined_3det\"][\"lr_acc_mean\"],\n                   all_results[c][\"combined_3det\"][\"rf_acc_mean\"]) for c in all_results]\n    summary[\"means\"][\"combined_5feat\"] = round(float(np.mean(c5_accs)), 4)\n    summary[\"means\"][\"combined_3det\"] = round(float(np.mean(c3_accs)), 4)\n\n    rf_vals = [rf_reference[c] for c in all_results]\n    summary[\"means\"][\"rf_19feat_reference\"] = round(float(np.mean(rf_vals)), 4)\n\n    out_path = os.path.join(args.output_dir, \"summary_v2.json\")\n    with open(out_path, \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(summary, f, indent=2, default=str)\n    print(f\"\\n  Results saved to {out_path}\")\n\n\nif
